ParlaMint-EE_2022-01-26_U33	Thank you, Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, This draft, 505 SE's first reading, held in the Committee on Monetary Affairs on 17 January. Keit Pentus-Rosimannus, Minister of Finance, and Toomas Honourable, Deputy Head of the Financial Services Policy Department of the Ministry of Finance participated. Indeed, the draft takes over two EU directives and amends the management and capital requirements of investment firms so that they take better account of the business models and risks of investment firms. And during this debate on 17 January, the Minister gave us an overview of the draft. Most important aspects have already been reported by the Minister. But yes, here is the question of changes in management and capital requirements, then of the increase in the limits on the compensation of deposits guaranteed by the Guarantee Fund. Okay. As investment firms and banks are currently subject to similar capital management requirements, they are not flexible enough for investment firms. The draft will make these requirements more proportionate for investment firms. This regulation as a whole takes better account of the risks and business models of different investment firms. Investment firms are divided into three categories by size: large, medium and smaller. The Minister has already talked about capital requirements; he is not going to repeat it here. This issue of performance fees was also mentioned. It is also important to stress that the amendments concern the simplification of the provision of investment services and the increase in the ceiling for the reimbursement of savings, which I mentioned. It was discussed. We had two issues in the Commission. My colleague, Aivar Kokk, asked why a performance fee should be paid to board members in shares and how other shareholders would be affected by this situation. And how does such remuneration be paid in companies that are not on the stock exchange? Mr Honourable explained that the requirement must be made out of the European Union's regulations and cannot be sidelined; they must be transposed. The purpose of this regulation is to link the commitment of the manager more closely with the company where he works. However, payment does not necessarily take place in shares; it can also be done in other securities. For example, an investment firm may issue bonds that do not directly give voting rights in an investment firm. So the choice to use the instrument remains up to the investment firm. Okay. Then I asked myself about the categorisation of these investment companies operating in Estonia. As far as banks are concerned, we have systemically important banks. Are investment firms here also systemically important, i.e. first class investment firms? ParlaMint-EE_2022-01-26_U45	We will move on to the 11th item, which is the first reading of the draft law on the prevention of money laundering and terrorist financing initiated by the Government of the Republic and the amendment of other laws (507 SE). I ask the Minister of Finance Keit Pentus-Rosimitud to present the speech chair of the Riigikogu. Please!
ParlaMint-EE_2022-01-26_U46	Thank you very much, Mr President of the Riigikogu. Happy Members of the Riigikogu! I will present to you the draft amendment to the Anti-Money Laundering and Terrorist Financing Act, which is due to the need to respond to the risks arising from the rapid development of financial technology. The scope of this bill is services similar to financial services, which can theoretically be used for money laundering or terrorist financing. Estonia was one of the first countries in the world to regulate and license providers of virtual currencies. The very rapid development of technology and innovation in the financial sector is something that we, as a country, decided that we wanted to be benchmarks here in 2017. In doing so, we also took responsibility for finding ways to mitigate abuses, in particular the risks of money laundering and terrorist financing. The popularity of virtual currencies has exploded, but in addition to enthusiasts and investors-developers in this area, fast and anonymous solutions have, unfortunately, become consciously used by criminals. In this context, the risk for Estonia, as the issuing State, has increased in a similar way. The aim of this draft is to reduce the likelihood of misuse that the current legal framework allows. The sector itself is international. However, very many companies issued in Estonia and authorised by Estonia have little connection with Estonia, which means that supervision is difficult at the moment. Inconsistent awareness in the sector has been identified, as well as lack of due diligence by virtual currency providers. The risk manifests itself as crimes of money laundering and terrorist financing, fraud and theft. For example, the demand for ransom payments by hackers who paralyze institutions' websites and applications has caused a lot of real damage. In addition, virtual currency services may be used to avoid international financial sanctions. And if we ignore the risks that have now arisen, unfortunately, this could have very sad consequences not only for this sector, but for the state as a whole, for the state as a whole. A realised money laundering scheme or terrorist financing through one of the virtual currency service providers licensed in Estonia would certainly cause serious reputational damage, the impact of which would be passed on much more widely and also to other sectors. This means that our reputation or our credibility in the eyes of our important partners will certainly be undermined. Of course, the risk associated with virtual currencies and virtual currency services has not only occurred in Estonia, but has been published in a large number of countries. The rapid increase in risk is marked by an increase in the popularity and availability of virtual currencies. It must be said that, also with regard to the COVID, part of crime has moved more online, often generating revenue in the form of virtual currency. The virtual currency sector is vulnerable to the average as operational requirements have been there and are currently lower than in the financial sector. Estonia responds to risk and these risks in three ways or in three waves. The draft before you proposes the most urgent changes necessary to mitigate the risks. These changes are starting to have an impact as of this year. The second step is the Act on crowdfunding and other investment instruments and virtual currencies. This is a draft which is still in the coordination circle and is already undergoing a broader reform, and a new, permanent regulatory solution will also be found for virtual currency services. The third step is the implementation of European Union law. However, as many people know in this Chamber, European Union law in this area is still in negotiations and these initiatives are likely to have an impact in three or more years' time. The aim and direction of all these three regulations, let us say, are the same, and the measures they take are all consistent with each other. So, along with the development of the sector, there is gradual regulation. This bill regulates in more detail the concept of providers of virtual currencies, obliges them to retain transaction data and to share them among service providers. This will enable research institutions, where appropriate, to restore the path of the virtual currency movement, which will play a central role in preventing crime within the meaning of international standards. In addition, we have a proposal to introduce additional operational requirements, requiring further reflection on the service provision, a stronger link with Estonia, which is necessary in order to enable our supervisory authorities to monitor these services in practice as well. Thirdly, we also foresee further changes of a more technical nature that are necessary for the Money Laundering Data Bureau. The possibility shall be given to carry out background checks on the staff and officials of the data bureau itself. Just to remind you that in the past, the Money Laundering Data Bureau (RAB) has been part of the Police and Border Guard Board. It is now an institution that operates under the authority of the Ministry of Finance. It is therefore also necessary to introduce this amendment in order to allow them to carry out, at the same time, a background check that was possible in the past. Non-profit organisations and foundations operating in countries subject to sanctioning regimes become obliged persons to reduce their exploitation by terrorist donors. When establishing identity, the debtor must continue to collect data on the means of communication of the natural person. Some of the changes necessary for the effective functioning of the database of beneficial owners will also be made. This is the kind of bill that I have to talk about, too, things that this bill does not do. The draft does not regulate technology, regulate not all services related to virtual currency, but only services capable of converting, maintaining, transferring and growing virtual assets. Perhaps these services are the scope of this bill, which has common features with financial services and which uses a theoretical opportunity to launder money or finance terrorism. No technology will be banned. Neither the possession of a virtual currency nor the restriction of turnover for the consumer in any way. The target group of the regulation is service providers who have been granted an Estonian licence. The draft also takes into account that not all other countries may have extended the international standard to providers of virtual currency. Nor do all virtual money bags or transactions necessarily have a different technical side to pass the information on to. The draft therefore also allows service providers to mediate transactions with service providers that do not yet apply the requirements and with separate wallets. However, in the case of such transactions, service providers need to monitor the transaction risk in real time. Technical solutions for this are already well in place and, in fact, in many cases have been introduced. As I mentioned earlier, having taken the pioneering role with Estonia once, the international community, not only active in this area, but also in this area, expects us to find regulatory options to implement international standards on the prevention of money laundering. And we are in this case the ones whose example other countries may begin to shape their own regulations and solutions. So that's why we can't stay here in a passive or waiting role. We have an active and cross-border sector on which we, as a country, have a responsibility, so that they are not also exploited in any way by the bad guys. Businesses that act honestly and create real innovation should not and should not suffer because of those who do not understand their role and responsibility as part of this new financial system and want to abuse it or do not want to cooperate meaningfully with supervision. I would also like to thank all those market participants, such as the Estonian Founders' Association, who are very active and meaningful in thinking about how we can achieve this result, which supports the objective that we have had when presenting the bill, that innovation, including innovation in the financial sector, is expected in Estonia, but the risks must be grounded and honest-minded developers, innovative service providers, developers should not be afraid that these evil-intentioned players will ultimately be fooled or, let us say, by using sufficiently precise regulation in any way to put their reputation in jeopardy. So, by presenting this draft and during these discussions, which have already taken place in the Committee on Monetary Affairs, we have overconfirmed from the Ministry of Finance and the Government that we are certainly prepared to act carefully, decisively, in cooperation with the Commission. If necessary, we will also develop amendments that would help achieve these objectives even more effectively, to mitigate risks, so that the draft is unambiguously understood by everyone who operates in this sector and the risks we identified last year in the risk assessment analysis can really be landed effectively. Thank you. I'll answer the questions.

ParlaMint-EE_2022-01-26_U49	Thank you. I have no way of commenting on what you've referred to or what you've identified. But this is a very serious matter, the risks really are great in this sector, not only are there certainly great opportunities and there are great opportunities for innovation. However, at present, part of the risks are related to this, as I said in my introduction, that there are a large number of companies operating with the licences applied for in Estonia, which do not really have much to do with Estonia. Which means that we will not actually be able to exercise any supervision over them. However, since the licence has been issued by Estonia, if it should become clear that they are acting for less honest purposes, the damage to their reputation and the damage that is actually caused by it will extend to Estonia first and foremost, and this risk we consider important to mitigate. That is why you have also been presented with a draft which specifically sets out the requirements for virtual service providers in a much more precise way. Among other things, it states that we would still like to make sure in the future that this activity is related to Estonia in one way or another when issuing the licence, not that the authorisation issued in Estonia is used as a tie leaf somewhere else, knowing that Estonia is not in a position to exercise supervision and control.

ParlaMint-EE_2022-01-26_U113	We are moving forward to the 12th item, which is the first reading of the Social Welfare Act initiated by the Government of the Republic and the Act amending other laws (510 SE). And I am pleased to ask for the speech chair of the Riigikogu, Minister of Social Protection Signe Riisalo. Please!
ParlaMint-EE_2022-01-26_U114	Okay, sorry, I got the mask off. Good morning. The amendment of the Social Welfare Act and, in fact, the amendment of the Child Protection Act, the Social Benefits Act for Persons with Disabilities and the Family Act. There are over 70 changes in this draft and, in conclusion, all of them contribute to reducing red tape, improving legal clarity and faster access to aid. The needs for change, as well as the solutions, have emerged from practice and have been discussed in a wide range of working groups, analysed in a number of memoranda, studies and also included in audits and reports, including in the Conclusions of the Convention on People with Disabilities. In two cases, this is a change resulting from the Supreme Court judgment. [[The manager's ringing the bell.]] On the subject of long-term care, the Ministry of Social Affairs has submitted four different memoranda to the Government of the Republic, and the draft amendment package includes, among other things, amendments to the first stage, which are a prerequisite for subsequent changes in the field of long-term care. Let it be said immediately that there are financial resources available for implementing the draft, and we will not ask for additional resources with this draft. The funds are in both the budgets of the Social Insurance Board and Tehnika. All ministries have, of course, been involved in the process of inclusion and coordination of the draft, but partners of great importance to us, such as the Advisory Board of the Estonian Leaders of Social Institutions, the Association of Special Welfare Providers, the Chamber of Disabled People and so on. The Association of Estonian Cities and Municipalities is always an important partner in the field of social welfare, although it did not share the same opinion with the Ministry during this coordination process with all the changes, but all the precise explanations are also available in the coordination table as an annex to this draft. The Ministry of Social Affairs has also explained to the Cities and Municipalities Association that the impact on local government was assessed in the sections of the draft, which are activities that have been specified in view of the current role of local government. In fact, in the sections of the draft, where the local authorities specified their responsibilities, an additional financial measure to support local authorities, which is not directly planned from the state budget but from the European Social Funds, is intended to provide local authorities with 1 million starting from 1 April, i.e. the date of entry into force, in order to prepare for the assessment of the needs of customers and better organisation of the provision of assistance, including further training for social workers, as well as the development of tools. And now for the changes in this bill. First, the so-called first stage of changes to the long-term care system. In the Commission, we also had more talk about the next steps planned by the Ministry and the Government this year. But with this bill, we're going to change the family law. While children, as well as grandchildren, have so far had maintenance obligations for their parents, this bill exempts secondary relatives from maintenance obligations. Simply put, a granddaughter is not obliged to pay for the care of a grandparent. And, as you can understand, there is much talk here about general care, where we know that nearly 80% of the cost of general care is borne by the client, his family and among them children and grandchildren. If you ask how many grandchildren there are today who cover maintenance obligations, the honest answer is that we do not actually have statistics on this, as general care is a service organised by the local government. However, I can give an example of the city of Tartu, where the part-financing of the city of Tartu has 300 general care clients and in these 300 cases only two grandchildren have been required to support themselves. This change makes sense, both because of family relations and because of the financial burden on grandchildren, but actually also serves the interests of the local government, because the regulation so far has put local government in a position where they have to make unnecessary efforts, bureaucracy to find out where the grandson is, where his grandson is, what his financial means are to support his grandparent and then demand it. Although helping people in need is also the responsibility of the local government today, the draft also specifies the task of the local government to identify the need for support for persons with a care load. If the need for assistance is identified, provision must be made for appropriate assistance, be it a person with maintenance needs or a maintenance load. This means that the periphery carers are also a clear target group here, for which the local authority must identify and then provide additional support. The earlier the informal carer has been supported, the more likely it will be to continue or begin to participate in the labour market. A caregiver's mental health problems and a drop in need will be prevented, as it is not rare that the caregiver himself proves to be in need. A person in need of care must be able to live in his or her own home for as long as possible. It is also a fine-tuned principle that we regulate, or actually specify, with amendments to the Welfare Act. The interest of all of us is that a person can age with dignity and that an institutional institution, including a general welfare institution, is indeed the last solution. Studies also show us that people's own preference is overwhelmingly to live at home when they need help and receive the necessary home services, whether they are involved in handling domestic activities or in personal care. The amendment also complements the principles of providing social welfare assistance and support with the principle that this home-style environment must also be provided for institutional care. And for the first time, we also regulate the concept of long-term care in law. The following amendments now concern this proactive approach. If we have built our welfare system in general since 1995 on the principle that the person in need must turn to the local government for help and apply for it, we have now directed more the local government to act proactively, or to turn to the people themselves, to find out their need for help. And this is not a new principle in law, but a more precise principle. Local authorities will be given the right to receive an overview of children living in their own region who have been asked for the degree of disability, not only for children who have been given the degree of disability, but also for those who have been asked for it, because we expect that there may be a need for help there. And in order to prevent the detection of disability severity, it is wise and reasonable and appropriate to help these children in the past through local government services and other assistance. But also adults who have been identified as disabled. And here's the point again: once a person has been identified as disabled, he doesn't have to turn to the local government himself, but he does a proactive first step to provide help by the local government, introducing to the person or his/her loved ones what the possibilities of receiving help in a particular local government are. Thus, the local government will have an overview of potential needers, children and adults, as well as older people, and it will be easier to plan the social services of the local government, both in the palette that is needed in this area and in the volume of services. In particular, it is also an instrument for the local government in budgetary planning. By creating an exchange of information between the state and local government, we try to identify the need for assistance for each child and family and adult as early as possible, so that the services provided can also be of a preventive nature. And, as we all understand, preventive services help to delay the difficult need for care, and this is easier for both the person and, in fact, for the local government and society at large. We also bring laws into line with case law. The list of documents to be submitted to the local government when applying for income support is amended. The list is supplemented in the light of the judgment of the Supreme Court, in which the Supreme Court has come to the conclusion that reducing the burden of debt in enforcement proceedings is not considered as a net income of the person, since the sums withheld in enforcement proceedings cannot be covered by the person in order to satisfy his or her primary needs. The second amendment to the Supreme Court's judgment concerns the determination of the severity of the disability and, if necessary, the principle has been specifically introduced here, that if a person cannot cope independently with the treatments and he/she needs the assistance to do so, so that the degree of disability should also be identified. The state has provided local authorities with resources to help children with severe and deep disabilities through the Support Fund every year. This has also led to the attitude of the local government to assist children who have already been disabled and who have, in particular, severe or deep disability. Of course, there is a desire for the services to reach children earlier, and therefore we will replace the criterion of a child with a child with a high need for help, regardless of whether the child needs help or not, and the scope of this assistance is large, it is possible to provide this state-funded resource from the support fund for the provision of services to the child and his family. The draft also sets a criterion for a social worker. If we already have a requirement for higher education for child protection workers, then the other social workers in the local government have not had such a requirement so far. In the future, a social worker must have a higher education degree that is a professional, or have acquired a professional certificate for a social worker. This is critical because the quality of social work and the skills of workers are, in fact, the things that help people, and a very large part of social work is precisely direct communication, knowledge, competence and skills. I am pleased to say that the Ministry of Social Affairs will also be able to support, once again, the training of social workers in the region of Ida-Virumaa, which will be the beginning of this month. And cut red tape and unnecessary business. People are exempted from providing data that are available in national databases when applying for income support. For example, you can use the land register here. We will also make it easier to identify the degree of disability in old-age and old-age people who are in an unchangeable or progressive state, enabling the degree of disability to be detected for a longer period than five years. So far, only children, now also old-age age, have had such an opportunity. I would stop here and be ready to answer questions from members of the Riigikogu.

ParlaMint-EE_2022-01-26_U155	Mr President, I would like to thank you very much. Honourable members of the Riigikogu! Last item on the agenda, the law amending the Family Benefits Act and other laws. Other laws that are amended are the Employment Contracts Act and the Social Tax Act in its course. The main objective of the draft and the majority of it is, in fact, the possibility of paying maintenance support to children who have been declared bankrupt in relation to the parent liable for maintenance. The aim is to reduce the poverty of parents raising children on their own and to ensure that they are better able to cope financially. If we think about how many children we have today who receive maintenance support, then the beneficiaries of maintenance support during court proceedings, the target group is 517 children, the beneficiaries of maintenance support during the enforcement procedure 5482. In the first case, the cost was EUR 152000, in the second case EUR 4,7 million. Now the additional target group, the ongoing maintenance aid for bankruptcy, is estimated to be 40 children. There were 18 children in 2017, 21 in 2018 and 30 in 2019. We have estimated and predicted 40 children of this size, which means an additional cost of EUR 50000. However, given that the entire budget of the Family Benefits Act is insanely large, this cost does not really require additional state budget applications. With the same bill, we will also improve the conditions for parental benefits and leave in the event of the child's stillbirth and in a situation where the child dies within 70 days of birth. Understandably, the extremely traumatic situation for the family, and the family also needs to be sentenced to a period of mourning and to be able to cope calmly with this grief. Not to mention, in the case of a woman who gave birth to a child, a mother, it is also important to restore her health. Another change made in the interests of equal treatment for parents is the year of birth of the child as the basis for calculating the upper limit of the parental benefit of one calendar month. Previously, this was based on the year in which the right to parental benefit arose, but here, for the sake of equal treatment of parents, this is the year of birth of the child, which is clearly the date that is unchanged. Now, the process of applying for parental leave, which is clearly and unambiguously laid down for applicants for parental leave and their employers, actually also provides for an electronic self-service environment where it is easy for parents to submit their applications for leave. The Social Insurance Institution already communicates with the employer accordingly. Other laws related to the Family Benefits Act and parental benefit. We equate a husband with a registered partner, these other laws, as mentioned earlier, are the Social Tax Act and the Employment Contracts Act. We will also clarify in the Social Tax Act by bringing some of the provisions from the level of the regulation to the level of the law, with the aim of being in line with good legislative practice, that we do not create new law at the level of the regulation, and that has also been the wish of the Chancellor of Justice. There are some technical improvements in this bill, but in the big picture we are talking about three things. Most important, the payment of a living or maintenance allowance for children where the parent who is obliged to support has been declared bankrupt; in the case of a stillborn child; and then issues relating to social tax. The rest is basically more technical. Thank you.

ParlaMint-EE_2022-01-26_U210	Good questioner, your time! Echo Kallas, please!
ParlaMint-EE_2022-01-26_U211	Thank you. It's hard to satisfy your wishes. First of all, we have done both general measures for all people and targeted measures. In addition, we have also taken a measure that will help all consumers who consume less than 650 kilowatt-hours of electricity, and that is the majority of consumers, because about 82 to 83% of electricity consumers consume less than 650 kilowatt-hours per month, and in this case the price cap is 120 euros per megawatt-hour or 12 cents per kilowatt-hour, which everything that goes over is reimbursed by the State. And that applies to everyone. It is also the same measure for gas consumers, so the price compensation is over 2.75 megawatts and 65 euros per megawatt-hour. The question of the district heating. Firstly, district heating goes down there, it goes under the targeted measure, and the rangers are also very different. If we look at them, some of the district heating companies who actually use wood chips, where this price has not increased significantly, and there are really regions where then the price of district heating has increased a lot, because there is gas as an input and this gas will then increase that price. So in this sense, the targeted measure concerns both electricity, gas and central heating and over-the-counter measures, as there are very different prices for district heating, the measures go over for electricity and gas because the price of gas has increased exceptionally high.

ParlaMint-EE_2022-01-26_U339	Thank you for your question. Unfortunately, your question is based on false assumptions. As regards the purpose of this bill, which is often missed, it is that in fact, it is not the rights of the private owner that are increased, but the decision-making power over whether or not the central decision-making, or the decision-making of the Environmental Board, is passed on to the local government. We have praised the municipalities in terms of this and the other way around, but when we talk about real life, they are often simply run over. And the lack of consideration of this draft shows, first and foremost, deep distrust of local authorities, or the structural structure of the country, which is laid down today at constitutional level. I firmly believe that more trust must be given to local governments, and this bill is one step in order for local authorities to have more rights. To tie it to the possibility that is, as tragically often expressed here, that it will soon be built all over the place, that the coastline will immediately change, and so on it is not true. Under today's law, half of Estonia's beaches have already been built and done on the basis of special permits granted either by the Minister or later by the Environmental Board. So all this aziotage around the bill is, how I say, illusional and doesn't correspond to the actual situation. As has been said yesterday, today's regime does not actually guarantee the protection of beaches, because the decision-makers are high and far away. In fact, this decision-making mechanism should be changed in order to manage the same protection of beaches precisely in the interests of local communities, rather than to do it from a purely distance, so for fun.

The exceptions were already mentioned by the islands and Narva-Jõesuu with 200. The Nature Protection Act, which we are talking about today, has been in force since 2004. In addition to the restrictions on construction ban zones, additional flood zones have been introduced. In addition, the definition of a line of the same height of one metre. Let it be said that the flood zone border may be from 20 meters to 200 metres and beyond, which is added to the construction ban zone. In addition, the term that forest areas bordering the beach must remain untouched by the saw. Perhaps there are actually many cases where the construction ban zone, the flood area and the forest bring together a group where the owner is not really able to do anything. He must look at the access, he must look at the building areas, whether they are reasonable to create a place that leads to the collection of waste water, which will lead to the construction of wells, which may lead to a change in the whole greening environment, until such time as to ensure that this shoreline is not in the garden. ParlaMint-EE_2022-01-26_U424	Let's continue on today's agenda. Our second item today is the draft amendment to the state border law initiated by the Isamaa Group, 489. This is a first reading and I call Urmas Reinsalu, a member of the Riigikogu, as rapporteur. Please!
ParlaMint-EE_2022-01-26_U425	Honourable representative of the people! Mr President, I would like to thank you very much. The faction of the Fatherland Party has initiated a bill to amend the law on our state border in order to resolve one of the acute legal gaps that affect our security, border security. Indeed, since last summer, the eastern border of the European Union, in particular Latvia, Lithuania and Poland, has been under attack from Belarus in the form of a massive and forceful attempt at illegal border crossings, and these countries have managed to block this force, including with the help of Estonia. But the situation with regard to our borders is that we have set up a temporary barbed wire barrier on a couple of ten kilometres of land border. And the rest of the land border, together with the land border, is about 300 miles [135 km], we're physically unblocked. We have to rely on a natural barrier in this possible attack situation, for example, when it comes to gender or difficult to penetrate. However, the situation is that our current law, as well as the interpretation of this right by the police department and the Ministry of the Interior, is basically that when there is an illegal attempt to cross the border, when we are dealing with a single case, police officers are obliged to detain, identify and apply sanctions for illegal border crossings. In the event that it now requests, so-called application for refugee status, requests international protection, proceedings shall commence with respect to that person which, hypothetically, should result either from the granting or rejection of the status of the beneficiary of that international protection, and, if the circumstances are objectively guaranteed, as is established in the country of origin, the original country of dispatch, in order to be able to remove that person as well. It's for a single person. The situation is made more complicated and this draft is designed to overcome this specific legal gap; we have seen these dramatic footage at, for example, the Polish border crossing point, where, in fact, a large number of people using force there, fortunately, not firearms, but stones, sticks, smoke rockets are actually trying to penetrate the country's territory. And then, in the case of specific countries, such as Poland, which we have seen, police officers, border guards will then try to prevent them from entering the country's territory. And now we imagine how, if this happens, for example, in the forest or somewhere, even in a situation where there is no physical barrier, which prevents it, and we know that this barbed wire fence, too, is actually quite quick to cross, even with a ladder, to throw potato bags on it, or it is possible to crush it quite quickly with the corresponding wire cutting equipment. And then the question is that if these people, this large group of people, are actually able to invade Estonia by force, suppose, five meters, suppose, 50 meters, in this situation, in which we do not have a physical border barrier, surely our border guard and police task and also the police have been given the task of preventing these people from crossing the state border, let's say they did not come, form shields using special means, then proportionally, water cannons, whatever, we saw from the practice of other countries. If they succeed, however, in crossing the state border in this amount, it is a paradox that our police officers do not have the right to push these people back from the Estonian border, in order to counter their attack in this form, but to detain them, to bring them to the detention house, to start proceedings against them. Procedures can be very long-term if people deny their country of origin, refuse to recognise it if they apply for international protection, it may be very difficult to force them to leave this country with procedures later on. And we actually have a police office itself in our own risk assessments that has established this limit when we talk about a large number of people, it is not hypothetical, as we have seen in the case of Lithuania, 5000 is the ability that we were also told in the Committee on Constitutional Affairs that, in a large way, it is possible to deal with many of them within the country, wherever they are isolated and the procedure is put into place. This question of thousands is not an academic problem with theoretical capabilities, because, well, thousands of these people actually arrived on Lithuania's territory. When we are talking about a hypothetical hybrid attack, isn't it, for example, by Russia or we are actually saying it, we are actually talking about the fact that it can be very numerous? And now the principle of describing this practical problem is that Poland and Lithuania have tried to solve it with the rapidly adopted amendments to the law, because this situation in the event of this type of crisis is undoubtedly, was also of a unique nature, so to speak, there must be an urgent response, and Poland and Lithuania have adopted a fundamental, if briefly describe this line of thinking, then it is that, in fact, if these people are invading, it is not the logic of international protection in the classical sense, but it is an attack. Then this is a security crisis, in which we cannot say that it is then within the scope of the regulation of international protection, within the scope of its scope, but it is clearly a national security problem. Therefore, those persons should be entitled to be forced back into the territory of the country of dispatch by police forces. From the format, that is, in fact, the two provisions, firstly, which we propose to Parliament when there is a massive attempted illegal border crossing, the Police and Border Guard Board is obliged, firstly, for security reasons to prevent it and refuse to accept applications for international protection, and, secondly, the principle that the Police and Border Guard Board is obliged, precisely for security reasons, to refuse to accept applications for international protection in the case of this type of mass illegal border crossing, and, rightly, to send foreigners who have crossed the border illegally back to the foreign country from which or through which they arrived in Estonia. And the legal vacuum of this problem was actually revealed in the summer. I wrote to the Prime Minister in early August, where I asked for this legal gap to be resolved without delay. I have also spoken to our police officers about this bill, of course since the summer. Police leaders also recognise the existence of a legal gap, which is a real practical problem. And it has in no way lost its acuteity in a situation where the war is knocking on the door of Europe, where we can be expected to be indeterminate, let us say, action involving the use of force, the use of force of different types by the Russian Federation in a situation where, in fact, there is a fundamentally dramatic and indeterminate confrontation between NATO and the Russian Federation, and we are a border country, and that means that all of this entails risks. And we have already tabled this bill in November because, in response to this call, the Prime Minister said that we are thinking, we are debating, we are thinking, but months have passed, and in a crisis situation we need to act decisively, quickly, there cannot be a very academic approach. There, in August, I also called on the Prime Minister to set up barriers to the border, quickly temporary barriers, months, months, barriers were finally temporarily installed, indeed, given that these barriers, I said, cover only a few decades of territory, from the land border line. In Estonia, in fact, if a leg, one leg establishes a sacred land according to its approach, right, then in fact, persons are already covered by the protection of the right of international protection. The Ministry of the Interior has already started developing the necessary amendments to the law and we will forward them to the Ministry for coordination. " It was also a question of when the Ministry of the Interior or the Government would come up with the corresponding draft, that we would be in a bit of a hurry, but this protocol also states when we discussed in the Committee on Constitutional Affairs that the development of this regulation is now the most important task in the Ministry of the Interior. But now to the meeting of the Committee on Constitutional Affairs. We discussed this draft amendment to the state border law initiated by the Isamaa Group (489 SE) on 18 January 2022. Ruth Annus, Head of the Department of Citizenship and Migration Policy of the Ministry of the Interior, and Ele Russak, Adviser were also invited. First of all, the problem that Urmas Reinsalu raised, and which already sounded in this Chamber, was that this in-house use mark on this document of the Government of the Republic. In fact, Urmas already replied that yes, this statement should be valid until this document has been approved by the Government of the Republic. There is no such reference here, but really, today he is public and this information has reached the meeting of the Committee on Constitutional Affairs. There was certainly no real concern here for a moment that it was as if this issue could not be discussed in the Chamber of the Riigikogu, because the document is for internal use. No, he was public at the time of the signing. But it was also negotiated in the Commission and finally clarified. Now to the substance of the bill. Urmas Reinsalu introduced this bill in the same colorful way he did it in this hall, so I will not go over this part again because we have already heard it. And then we'd go to the questions and the debate. Paul Puustusmaa said that this bill was absolutely necessary in itself and he supported it, but in the interests of a common understanding, he still had some questions. He examined what was happening at the moment of the massive illegal border crossing in order to be able to implement these standards, and he also wanted to know what the maximum way to prevent: is the use of firearms, for example, allowed or not? Paul Pulpland also examined what would happen if the country to which we wish to send the border crossingrs back was not willing to accept them. Urmas Reinsalu replied that the primary assumption for mass border crossings is that Estonia can still hold these people on the border line, but if we can no longer hold them and there are many people, then the mass attack will begin. It is certainly not a case of individual cases where a person has simply sneaked across the border and the police are not aware of his border crossings, but of situations where there is a substantive appearance of an attack, where force is being tried to cross the border and the police are not able to repel those persons from the border. With regard to the firearm, he added that the use of the firearm is proportionate when the attack involves the use of a firearm or a type of weapon that endangers the lives of border guards. But he believes there are still ways to use force that doesn't lead to casualties. Then Paul Puustusmaa also said that he had the understanding that the use of the Convention on the Status of Refugees was possible in a situation where there was a first country to which refugees were coming, but Estonia was probably not the first country, because these refugees came from somewhere further away. He said that if, for example, people come from Iraq through Russia to Estonia, Estonia is not the first country and these conditions and obligations should not extend to Estonia. He asked if he misunderstood. Urmas Reinsalu explained that one thing is the universal applicability of this convention internationally, but in fact here its interpretation has been given by the European Union. The rules governing the grant of international protection have been laid down in the internal law of the European Union and, according to it, the first country to be considered to be the country through which a person has first reached the territory of the European Union. Toomas Kivimägi said that he thought Urmas Reinsalu would agree with him that the law on the national border would apply to the crossing of the external borders of the European Union to the extent that the Schengen Borders Code is not regulated. He examined whether the draft submitted by the Fatherland was in line with the Convention on the Status of Refugees or whether it would be an obstacle or even contrary to the Convention. Urmas Reinsalu replied that the draft was fully in line with the Convention on the Status of Refugees. Ruth Annus was then given the floor at the Ministry of the Interior and she noted that there are already quite a number of methods in Estonian law that enable Estonia to cope with the emergency caused by mass immigration. In this case, the director-general of the PBGB will decide when an emergency occurs. It also pointed out that the Government of the Republic considers it necessary, however, to clarify the applicable law, such as the role of judicial proceedings. The whole issue of the draft therefore requires a more broad approach. In addition, the European Union has begun to regulate its right to instrumentalised migration, such as the creation of concrete measures for the mass immigration situation that has taken place from Belarus towards Poland, Lithuania and Latvia. The EU has also launched a regulation setting out the conditions under which the EU as a whole can respond. Perhaps this question here was also on behalf of the Fatherland ambassadors, who have left the hall now, but not only have we, at national level, the Ministry of the Interior, taken up this issue and, if necessary, changed or worked in this direction, that yes, also at European Union level, this problem and European law are already being dealt with. Here's the answer. Ruth Annus explained that the Ministry of the Interior has also wondered how it would be best to organise the issue of mass immigration in order to comply with international law and existing European Union law. If the European Commission has come up with a real legislative proposal, then a Member State cannot normally regulate the same area. However, it has to be admitted that the processing of European Union legislation is not very urgent. Ruth Annus said that, on the basis of the foregoing considerations, the Government of the Republic considered draft 489 to be a step in the right direction, but in this matter, amendments to other laws need to be developed and the text proposed in draft 489 amended. It may be discussed whether this should be a matter of security, which should enable people to be excluded from the external borders. It is more of a question of migration within the EU. Ms Russak then spoke, who said that in 2019 she had also been able to introduce to the Committee on Constitutional Affairs amendments to the law on the obligation of departure and refusal of entry and the law on the granting of international protection to foreigners. These changes were made in order to better deal with the emergency caused by mass immigration and were adopted in 2020. The most important change was already foreseen at the time that the PBGB must have sufficient time to determine who border workers are dealing with, what reasons and motives these persons have come to Estonia and then decide whether the persons are to be returned or should be directed to international protection proceedings. For this purpose, the Act created the possibility for persons to be detained for up to seven days with the permission of the Administrative Court in order to carry out proceedings. It was also foreseen that the PBGB would be able to submit such requests for detention on a list rather than on an individual basis as in normal circumstances. In addition, the courts were given the opportunity to make an Alien Detention Order without any descriptive and reasoned knowledge. An opportunity was created to introduce all detention facilities in Estonia, including prisons, in order to limit the services provided to persons. Ele Russak also noted that events at the external borders of Belarus and the European Union have led all Member States to re-examine their laws. The Ministry of the Interior has also carefully considered whether the existing measures are sufficient to cope with the hybrid situation. The Ministry of the Interior considers that in order to prevent and combat mass border crossings, the law on law enforcement contains measures that can be applied by the PBGB. The PBGB can apply all preventive and preventive surveillance measures, including coercion. However, it does feel that it would be very sensible to revise the sectoral laws once again in order to allow for faster return of border crossingrs. He also pointed out that the possibility of derogating from European Union law could be considered and that foreigners who have entered the country could be sent back as a matter of urgency, ignoring the legal channels set up for this purpose. This right has also been confirmed in 2020 by the European Court of Human Rights, which took the view that countries may prohibit an alien from entering the country, including potential applicants for international protection if he or she enters the country in a place not designated for that purpose, and would have been able to lodge his or her application for international protection at the border crossing points designated by the country. The Ministry of the Interior would like to grant the PBGB the right to provide for border crossing points where the person has the right to submit an application for international protection. Similarly, in a situation where a person ignores legal entry channels, the PBGB must have the possibility to promptly return the person. Urmas Reinsalu said that we are talking about somewhat different categories. SiM was talking about speeding up expulsion procedures, and this is all very commendable. This concerns persons who are already on the territory of Estonia, and proceedings are being opened against them. Urmas Reinsalu noted that draft 489 concerns a practical legal gap relating to the prevention of immigration at the border. Ruth Annus replied that the law in force did not provide for a written procedure in this case, but that if a person had crossed the border illegally, whether or not he had applied for international protection, he would be returned from the border to the country from which he came to Estonia across the border. Urmas Reinsalu asked whether the person would be returned to a judicial procedure in which the person would be transferred to the detention centre and the court would rule on him. Ruth Annus replied that the person would be returned to the country where he came from in the same place that he was detained. This tactic was used by Poland and Lithuania. Foreigners are not allowed to cross national borders, both under international law and EU law, in a place not designated for that purpose. Urmas Reinsalu still noted that his question is about the law in force, that if a large number of persons have already crossed the border to the territory of Estonia, how should it be dealt with in accordance with the law in force. Ele Russak replied that, under current law, crossing the external borders is considered a danger of escape and that the risk of escape is the basis for the detention of the person. Detention is possible for more than 48 hours only with the permission of the administrative court. The PBGB is therefore obliged to detain the person and try to send him out immediately. Deportation is subject to an administrative act, which means that the PBGB must make and enforce a letter of departure. If this cannot be done within 48 hours, the PBGB must be authorised by the administrative court to apprehend the person. Urmas Reinsalu then said that he was governed by the law currently in force and that the law in force was inadequate in the light of recent events. If there is a battle situation where there is a mass border crossing, then Estonian police officers must have the right to push these people back from the border. Toomas Kivimägi asked whether the Ministry of the Interior considered that there should be a different legal regime depending on whether the application for international protection was made at the designated border crossing point or if the person had crossed the border at a place not designated for that purpose. Is there agreement with the initiators of the SIM bill on this point? Ruth Annus replied that no one anticipated a hybrid attack like that in Poland and Lithuania. The situation has changed and the international community has realised that people can in principle be used as such to achieve political objectives and destabilise the EU. If SiM could have foreseen such a situation, the Ministry of the Interior would have requested that the amendments in question, which are currently planned, be dealt with as a matter of urgency as early as last year. And here, too, Ruth Annus said that the development of this regulation is now the most important task in the Ministry of the Interior. Oudekki Loone asked the same question as the natural disaster in this Chamber and received the same answer. I'm not gonna go over this again. He also asked about the use of weapons, and in fact, Paul had already received the answer to what I read here in Puustusmaa. Toomas then summed up the topic of Kivimägi, considering that, in his view, too, refusal to accept applications for international protection should not be provided for as an obligation, as currently stated in the draft, but rather as an option for the PBP. Toomas Kivimägi said that he would propose that the first reading of the draft be completed because he wanted this subject to remain topically up. This is an issue that needs the intervention of the legislator. In the last paragraph of the opinion of the Government of the Republic, the Government of the Republic, in the identity of the Minister of the Interior, admits that it is actually necessary to clarify the law. At the same time, it is also important for the Government of the Republic to note that changes can be made in accordance with the international obligations that Estonia has assumed. Toomas Kivimägi noted that he did not therefore support quite one draft text. At the same time, however, all parties recognise that there is a problem and that there is an urgent need for a solution. On the basis of this debate, the following decisions were taken: to propose that the draft be placed on the plenary agenda on 25 January, today, and to propose that the first reading of the draft be terminated and that Mr Toomas Kivimägi, Chairman of the Committee on Constitutional Affairs, be appointed as the representative of the lead committee. On 24 January, the Committee on Constitutional Affairs met again and since Toomas Kivimägi was unable to report a debate today, the Committee on Constitutional Affairs appointed Mart Võrklaev, i.e. mine, as a member of the Committee on Constitutional Affairs, on 24 January. That's what made the decisions, and now I'm waiting for the questions.

ParlaMint-EE_2022-01-26_U503	We can move on to the third item. This is the draft law on business register initiated by the Government of the Republic, number 493 SE, we are dealing with first reading and the rapporteur is Minister of Justice Maris Lauri. Please!
ParlaMint-EE_2022-01-26_U504	Mr President, I would like to thank you very much. Honourable members of the Riigikogu! The purpose of the Commercial Register Act is to harmonise the regulation of the register of all legal persons governed by private law. There are currently a wide variety of legislation, as well as a wide range of registers and sub-registries. This draft establishes a single register and a single set of rules instead of all these different regulations. In addition, legislation and register keeping will be updated to suit the electronic register and will also be made technologically neutral. In addition, a number of changes relating to registers are made based on the concept of company law presented by the Company Law Review Group, namely Chapter 4 thereof, which was then presented on 15 September 2018. What, then, will be changed in the register? Currently there is not only a register of non-profit organisations and foundations, but also a number of sub-registers: religious associations, trade unions, housing associations, land improvement associations. It will become part of the business register and become part of a single regulation. This will include all private legal entities, including self-employed persons, in the Commercial Register. The whole register procedure will become uniform and understandable. Maybe you don't have to look in different laws for how to act on one occasion or another. The records are divided into three, and they are currently divided into three. Registration card details: here the data that can then be entered is recorded. And in the case of special laws, it's where you write down what's supposed to be written. Here, for example, which data must be entered in the register for housing associations, but which may not need to be entered in the case of religious associations, for example. Public file data, currently referred to as a business file in the Commercial Register, here are other public data. The data in the registry file, it also currently has the same name, this part, and here is the data that is not directly publicly available immediately. In the future, the details of the shareholders of the private limited liability company will also be entered in the register. At present, the shareholders' list is held by the board of directors of the private limited company and, if anyone enters this information in the business register, it is informative, and they may not be up to date, these data. If this information now becomes "legal content" on the commercial register card, it means that if transactions are made with parts of a private limited company, the data in that commercial register can be considered to be correct. So if there's a thing that's not included, it's still what's in the business register that's gonna be right, so there's gonna be a chance to get good faith, and there's gonna be less chance of fraud. The draft also extends the possibility of monitoring the registrar. Unfortunately, as you know, there are quite a few problems with the accuracy, reliability and non-submission of data. The draft creates a number of possibilities for these data collections to be properly implemented and for monitoring and also for drawing attention to people who look at the data in the business register, that it may not be the right data, or that the company is in some kind of problematic situation. What, then, will be allowed to do? An e-business register may be used to indicate any difficulties in contacting. A normal situation, for example, that we have to write down, do not work, do not have active use or do not respond. Information relating to fines relating to a business register shall be recorded in relation to a related natural person and shall be recorded only if there are sufficient numbers to prevent a single fine from being recorded. What's that? For example, the chairman of the board. And what can it be useful for? We know that in the case of troubled companies, there are often so-called "tankers" who are the same people, and if they now have these notes, then if you look at the business records and you can see that the board of directors of this business register is a person who already repeatedly has comments that he does not comply with his obligations, then there is probably a warning that it may not make sense for this company to enter into a transaction. In addition, shareholders of a private company may be fined if the annual report is not submitted. It often happens that there is no manager and then there are only so-called shareholders, or owners, that then there is also the opportunity to turn this fine towards shareholders. Not including reorganisation if one of the parties concerned has or has not submitted an annual report. There is also a possibility, which is often used to make malicious transactions and, for example, to transfer assets and say, to get rid of obligations. In addition, a legal person may be removed from the register if the annual report is not submitted and there is no contact person. The submission of a contact person is mandatory. But this possibility, because it's a pretty tough option, there are two conditions, actually three. First, this legal person has no assets, has no legal proceedings and, for example, if they are presented in annual accounts, within three years it will be possible to reinstate that legal person in the register. The draft also establishes group rules. A little explanation. In particular, parent companies may issue instructions to the members of the management bodies of a subsidiary, which may be in the interests of the parent company, but may oppose the interests of the subsidiary. It is known that the management body may be penalised if they make decisions that are to the detriment of the company. In such cases, where such decisions are taken by the parent company, the parent company shall be liable for its decision if the subsidiary is in a difficult economic situation where insolvency is likely to occur. In other words, the managers of the subsidiary are not responsible if the parent company directs and forces them to make perhaps not the most rational decisions in the view of the subsidiary's interests. The draft provides for the abolition of the minimum capital requirement for private limited liability companies. At present, the minimum capital is EUR 2500, which has been essentially the same since 1995. He was 40,000 kroons back then. At the same time, we realise that its substantial value has been considerably reduced. And I would like to remind you that, from 2011, it will be possible to set up private limited companies without a contribution. In principle, if we create a company, the company's capital should correspond to the risks associated with the company, and this should be considered by shareholders when starting a business and also when carrying out a business. There are certainly companies where the capital needs are small, perhaps this 2500 even seems to be a lot, and there are certainly companies where it is essential that this capital is significantly higher. So, in a sense, it directs owners to think a little about how to capitalise a company both when it is set up and when it is needed to raise capital. Smaller changes are allowed, for example, to book a business name for up to six months, but in justified cases the entry can also be made on a specific date, for example if there are mergers or reorganisations, which may be more complex transactions, and where there may also be links to different parties involved or activities with different business names and market names. In addition, the rules on accounting and auditing of legal persons are brought into line with the Accounting Act and the Audit Audit Law. It also simplifies the winding-up of a single-member private limited-liability company, as there is no need to further protect shareholders. Due to the large volume of development of information systems, this draft would enter into force in three stages. The general entry into force would take place from February 2023, changes to the list of shareholders on 1 September 2023, and entries allowing the reservation of a business name and a fixed date from March 2024. Thank you for your attention.

ParlaMint-EE_2022-01-26_U515	We are going from here to the fourth item on the agenda, which is the first reading of draft law 504 on protection initiated by the Government of the Republic, such an interesting draft law. ParlaMint-EE_2022-01-26_U516	Mr President of the Riigikogu. Honourable members of the Riigikogu! The purpose of the law on the protection of whistleblowers is to ensure protection for those people who become aware of and report some kind of violation. In order to commit an infringement, it is illegal, but also inaction, as well as a situation where the purpose of the legislation is to commit an act that is contrary to what, in itself, may not be formally an offence. For example, to explain the matter, when a child defender does or does not do anything to protect a child, even though he or she does not have this legal obligation in itself, he or she has the task of dealing with child protection, but he or she fails to do something, which is why the child's well-being suffers. The infringement may take place in both the public and private sectors. The first question is certainly why such a notifier needs protection. It often happens that people notice a violation of the law, but they do not report it. It's also what the lawbreakers expect. First of all, that they would not be noticed, their actions, and if so, they would not be reported. Why is it not reported, especially when we are talking about a violation of employment, an infringement that has been discovered in a professional situation, which is precisely what this law is about? It is feared that this will be followed by professional harassment, punishment, for example by losing pay or promotion, but perhaps also by losing a job. However, information is needed, because if the infringement is not reported, the infringement cannot be brought to an end, especially if it is done deliberately. What violations, above all, can we address here? Environmental violations, such as trash, are spilled somewhere, are ordered to pour under the forest, work safety requirements are not respected on the construction site, but certainly corruption cases where money is transferred to their own or more favourable conditions are created, perhaps receiving something in return or God knows what to do. The law establishes a system of confidentiality for the notifier, which is what should be the first to protect the notifier. In addition, safeguard measures will be put in place for the notifier when his/her person comes out, and the possibility of punishing the notifier for bullying will be created, but there will also be the possibility of punishing people who knowingly give false information, i.e. using information for other purposes, perhaps for bullying someone else. According to the draft law, the infringement notification system should be of a three-step nature. First internal, then external and then, thirdly, public information. The purpose of the notification of the infringement is to put an end to the infringement and this can be done as quickly as possible by the authority or undertaking in which the infringement takes place. The following interventions can be undertaken by the supervisory authority. That is precisely the logic that this three-step system has been built up. The internal information channel must be set up in all government agencies, constitutional institutions, other public authorities, municipalities with more than 10000 inhabitants or with more than 50 employees, as well as in municipal authorities and other legal entities with more than 50 employees. At least one secure communication channel shall be established. This may be, for example, an email address, a mailbox, which may also be managed by a contractual partner, such as a law firm. The external information channel must be set up by public authorities which supervise or handle offences in a particular area. Public information should be made available after the unsuccessful completion of the previous stages or phase. This is because the infringement can still be brought to an end, in particular by the authority or the supervisory authority. In addition, it is possible to establish at the level of whether the infringement actually occurred, i.e. to prevent maliciously coming to the public immediately with an alleged infringement that did not actually occur. They're still gonna review and assess whether it really happened. It also makes it possible to avoid undue reputational damage, because it is undoubtedly more difficult to explain afterwards that nothing really happened than to prevent such a thing. However, the possibility remains to go immediately to public information, but this is possible in certain limited cases. Public information is not only about going to the press, but also about communicating via social media, for example. What are the conditions for notification, for which this protection arises only in situations where the notification takes place in a situation where this condition of notification has been observed? Firstly, as has been said, there must be a secure communication channel that ensures the confidentiality of the notifier. Confidentiality is not, in fact, the same as anonymity. Following the notification, the notifier must be informed within seven days that the notification has been received and is being handled. There shall be up to three months to deal with the substance, up to six months to be informed outside the institution in justified cases. The notifier must be informed as soon as possible of the outcome of the procedure, even if nothing has actually happened or if so, what has been done. If the notifier is not informed or nothing is done to bring the infringement to an end, the notifier shall be entitled to use the following communication channel: The notifier shall be protected only if he has undergone the notification as described above. The notifier shall, on the basis of objective facts, believe that the infringement will take place. The notifier, when his/her person comes out, shall not be subject to any pressure or threat of pressure. The disclosure of the identity of the notifier is also punishable. The penalties are as follows, they are misdemeanours: for a person up to 300 daily base units, in this case up to EUR 1200, and for a legal person up to EUR 400000. Case abuse is handled by the Police and Border Guard Board, except in cases where the infringement appears in procedures conducted by the kapo. It is also important that notification of an infringement does not preclude the notifier from being held liable for its infringement. According to the proposal, the law could enter into force on 1 June of this year, but companies will be given time to set up internal communication channels until 17 December 2023. Thank you!

So these are the opportunities created by this law. This law does not deal with the way in which one or the other of the offences is dealt with specifically.
